Source x19–x30 preservation is kernel-owned as selected below; the non-returning export-wrapper entry/linkage and x0..x17 compiler declarations are selected above; x18 is caller-volatile as selected above; FP/SIMD is prohibited/trapped for this slice as selected above; non-payload GPR/NZCV scrubbing is selected above, with implementation/validation pending. No implementation or end-to-end validation is claimed.

**Possible later extension (option B, not selected):** a six-`u64` result payload may be reconsidered if component interfaces need more register-only results. It is not part of the current ABI or a prerequisite for Bounce. Pursuing it requires a separate contract decision and coordinated PPC completion transport, wrapper/decoder and test updates; do not reserve or silently enable extra result words now.

Maintainer-selected directions (2026-09-28 onward) for the open D7 PPC execution details; research evidence in [lifetime-and-authority §7](lifetime-and-authority.md). These are contract directions, not implementation claims; exact wire schemas remain open.

- **Interrupt-kernel discipline.** The kernel keeps one stack per core; a blocked or migrated Thread's continuation never lives on a kernel stack. The SVC wait path copies the continuation into Thread storage before selecting another Thread, rewrites the transient frame, and unwinds normally to `ERET`; the former stack-carried park/jump mechanism is removed. Capability entry admits only execution-stack origins (trusted fixture `CurrentSp0` or lower `AArch64`) before dispatch, rejecting kernel-stack `CurrentSpx` with `InvalidDomain` before wait admission. Kernel-mode traps retain only nested transient frames and cannot schedule or block. PPC likewise requires the continuation to be Thread-resident before any switch. Interrupt-time work that cannot complete on the per-core stack is deferred to return-to-user safe points (Fluke preempter pattern).
- **Thread-resident bounded invocation stack.** Each Thread carries a bounded array of kernel-owned continuation records — per record: the caller's AddressSpace identity, saved user SP, saved user PC, and return metadata. Depth exhaustion is rejected at call time with a defined error to the source, never a panic. Storage, record layout, and depth are selected below; the top index lives in the Thread entry (a per-core cache with commit-on-switch is an SMP-later refinement, since the nucleus has no per-CPU state today).
- **Invocation-stack storage, record, and depth (storage/depth selected 2026-09-29; source-register preservation option B).** The bounded invocation stack is a fixed-depth array of continuation records **inline in the Thread's pool entry** (Composite's shape): no separate pool and no runtime allocation — the pool carve is boot-supplied and its thread capacity is the libOS `PoolCapacities.threads` knob. Depth is a kernel constant **16**. The source SPSR is also saved in each record (maintainer-approved): exception origin alone does not retain interrupt masks or the complete return execution state, and Return must not inherit the target's SPSR. Kernel-owned preservation of source x19–x30 adds twelve `u64` words (96 B), changing the projected record size from 48 B to **144 B**. Depth 16 therefore projects **2304 B** of record storage per Thread, 1536 B more than the former projection; four Threads need 9216 B for the record arrays alone, before other Thread fields and pool metadata. Verify the actual record/Thread layout, alignment, complete carve size and per-Thread page fit when implemented. These are projections, not measured layout validation; do not force the whole multi-Thread pool into one page or retain old backing/accounting constants. The depth constant lives in the shared catalogue so kernel and userspace agree. The record holds the source AddressSpace identity (`ObjectId`), the source return PC (ELR_EL1 — resume after the `Call` SVC), the source user SP, an 8 B per-call time stamp, the source SPSR, the source exception origin, and the twelve source integer registers x19–x30. Under the selected one-keytable-per-AddressSpace model, the source table is recovered from the source AddressSpace identity and the target table from the target AddressSpace; no per-record keytable field is needed. The record holds no target AddressSpace field (that is `Thread.address_space` while migrated). **Selected source register/linkage preservation (option B):** on an admitted Call, the kernel copies source x19–x30 from the transient saved exception frame into the new Thread-owned continuation record before committing migration. Successful Return restores those exact twelve words from the top record together with source AddressSpace/SP/PC/SPSR/origin, while independently delivering SUCCESS/r0/r1 in x0/x1/x2. A recoverable pre-commit Call rejection leaves source x19–x30 and existing records unchanged. Nested Calls save each immediate source's own register snapshot; restoration never trusts target code, its stack, or direct Return to preserve the values. No source 96-byte spill/restore stub is required by the PPC ABI. Storage remains inline in the boot/libOS-supplied Thread pool backing, without runtime kernel allocation or a per-Thread kernel stack. This changes the former compact-record direction; x18 is separately selected as caller-volatile (not saved here). FP/SIMD is prohibited/trapped for the current integer-only slice; non-payload GPR/NZCV scrubbing is selected above, with implementation/validation pending. Target export-entry linkage uses the selected non-returning wrapper, and target-entry SPSR controls inherit the saved source status except for cleared NZCV. Complete architectural-state isolation remains open, while status inheritance, GPR/NZCV scrubbing and preservation implementation/tests remain pending. Target function arguments/return values use their eventual ABI-defined live registers at the `Return` SVC. Pushing past depth 16 is rejected at call time with `CapError::NestingDepth`, wire status `NESTING_DEPTH = 33` in `x0`, current/maximum saved-continuation count in `x1`, and zero in `x2`: `(33, 16, 0)` for the full depth-16 array, not attempted depth 17. This is a recoverable pre-commit failure preserving the source and existing continuation records — never a panic, never a raw errno, not `PoolExhausted` and not Return-underflow fault delivery. The shared status/error implementation is pending. Nesting is Composite-style (a bounded nest), not KeyKOS-style serialized target execution.
- **Keytable identity for migrated code (selected 2026-10-02).** Each AddressSpace has exactly one keytable, shared by every Thread executing in that AddressSpace. `Call` switches both the translation context and keytable to the target AddressSpace's; `Return` restores the source AddressSpace, from which the source keytable is resolved. The source AddressSpace already in the continuation record therefore suffices; no separate keytable field or target-table field in the Invocation is selected. Composite provides the component-level `captbl` precedent: threads select the table of their active logical component. This is not a one-table-per-hardware-address-space rule: co-resident Composite components can retain distinct tables, with current-component selection reconstructed from kernel/user invocation state. Vesper's selected rule instead binds one table to its AddressSpace protection boundary; merely co-locating procedures there does not select separate component-local authority namespaces. Legacy merged-domain and current ULK mechanisms, and their confinement limits, are distinguished in lifetime-and-authority §7. The association and caller lookup are implemented through the immutable carved-table binding described under Thread and AddressSpace contracts, with no per-Thread table field and no runtime kernel allocation. Bounce names a distinct AddressSpace with its own table; the trusted two-Thread wait/resume fixture switches real independent roots/ASIDs and resolves capabilities through the selected Thread's current AddressSpace. Its code uses the retained linked Kicktest low-half image, its execution stack uses the high direct map, and nucleus handlers/vectors/traps use the invariant high kernel map. This validates the hardware/table-selection prerequisite, not same-Thread PPC Call/Return.
- **Per-call time accounting (selected 2026-09-29).** Each continuation record carries an 8 B stamp taken at the `Call` transition. At `Return`, the elapsed time since the top record's stamp is attributed **to the source Thread itself** (its own DCB consumed time), which is the selection for now: the record's presence at depth greater than zero marks that the target is running on the source's behalf, while the broader attribution semantics — whose budget across a hierarchy, how a scheduler observes depth or the current AddressSpace, per-target-domain splits — are deferred decisions. Charging discipline (charge before switching, never roll back) and a real clock land with Time/D8, which is still an excluded sketch (`current_time_ns()` returns zero today): the field and the transition stamps are recorded now and the arithmetic stays inert until Time exists. Charging remains thread-scoped in the meantime, which all three reference implementations show is correct by construction because the payer follows the thread.
- **Fully interrupt-kernel blocking (selected 2026-09-29; implemented 2026-10-03).** The blocked-wait continuation is Thread-resident too: `ExecutionContext::Parked` owns a `SavedContext` and pending-record identity, while `NotStarted` owns its initial `SavedContext`. The kernel-stack frame address and `park_and_switch`/`resume_parked_context` jumps are removed; `park_and_resume` installs the prepared translation context and rewrites the active transient frame after its scheduling lock ends. A Thread carries at most one parked continuation (a Thread-resident saved user context: the register set plus SP, ELR, SPSR, and the exception origin), distinct from the invocation stack; a blocking wait records the waiter's identity in its pending record, wake-up restores the parked continuation from Thread-resident state with the terminal result words injected, and teardown simply drops it. No kernel-stack bytes carry a continuation beyond the active trap. The saved context is 280 B/alignment 8; the transient exception frame is 288 B/alignment 16, with Rust/assembly size and offset assertions. Thread/pool backing is type-derived and page fit is asserted; the depth-16 PPC array is not yet implemented, so its combined layout still needs verification. The pending-record single-terminal-transition machinery is preserved while only its parking carrier changes. Kickstart enters trusted EL1t with the retained low execution stack reserved in boot accounting and a distinct shared high SP_EL1 trap stack. IRQ/preemption safe-point machinery and protected EL0 execution remain unimplemented.
- **CreateInvocation stack-extent wire encoding (selected option A).** `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation` operation `3` uses `x2` nonzero function address, `x3` destination KeyTable capability, `x4` vacant destination slot, `x5` stack base, `x6` exclusive stack end, and `x7` positive minimum downward headroom in bytes (selected option A for headroom encoding). The minimum is a direct `u64` byte count, not a size exponent: this permits non-power-of-two requirements and avoids exponent decoding. The published byte extent is `[base, end)`, rejecting `base >= end`; base-plus-length encoding is not used. Explicit boundaries match the subsequent range check without base-plus-length arithmetic. This settles the extent representation; the separately selected Call-time SP endpoint/headroom rule is below. The extent boundaries and minimum headroom must each be multiples of 16 bytes. Construction rejects a minimum larger than the extent before installing the capability with `InvalidStack` reason `MinimumHeadroomTooLarge`, reporting the submitted minimum with selected status 32. The active kernel constructor and six-input userspace wrapper implement this schema, with stored validated extent/headroom and real-SVC construction tests; the zero-reserved schema is removed.
- **Call-time SP endpoints and minimum headroom (selected option A with headroom constraint).** For the published byte extent `[base, end)`, require 16-byte-aligned `SP`, `base < SP <= end`, and `SP - base >= M` for an agreed positive minimum downward stack headroom `M`. An empty descending stack may start at `SP = end`; `SP = base`, and SPs with less than `M` bytes below them inside the declared extent, are rejected before any push or context switch. The target component/export setup supplies positive `M` at `CreateInvocation`, and the Invocation payload stores it; the kernel enforces this Invocation-specific requirement at each Call. This selects a logical constructor input and stored requirement, not a fixed kernel/ABI 4 KiB floor. `M` is supplied as a direct `u64` byte count in `x7` and must be a positive multiple of 16 bytes that fits the extent (`M <= end - base`). This replaces the reserved-zero `x7` contract, with no compatibility encoding. Validate ordering before subtracting, avoiding overflowing `base + M`. This is numeric headroom within the declared extent only, not proof of mapped/writable backing, stack-slot exclusivity, or sufficient space for arbitrary target code. These failures use implemented `InvalidStack` status 32 and the reason catalogue/IDs below. `InvocationStackExtent::validate_sp` implements and tests this numeric predicate independently; actual Call admission/push/switch remains unimplemented.
- **Stack-extent/headroom alignment (selected option A).** Require `base`, `end`, and the target-published positive minimum headroom `M` to each be multiples of 16 bytes, matching the already-selected 16-byte Call-time SP alignment. Do not round malformed inputs or require 4 KiB/page-aligned boundaries: sub-page extents and non-power-of-two sizes remain supported. Alignment violations are rejected explicitly with the field-specific `InvalidStack` reasons below before capability installation or Call push/switch, using selected status 32. Construction rejects `M > end - base` before capability installation, as specified below. Construction and the independent numeric SP helper implement and test these alignment rules; no live Call validation is enabled.
- **Construction-time minimum-headroom fit (selected option A).** `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation` rejects `M > end - base` before capability installation, preserving destination-slot state and authority. Check `base < end` before computing the difference, so malformed extents cannot underflow the fit calculation. Equality is accepted: when `M == end - base`, only the aligned empty-stack top `SP = end` satisfies the Call-time stack predicate. Together with the selected positive/aligned minimum and user-range extent checks, this prevents construction of a numerically uncallable stack contract; it does not prove mappings, writability, stack-slot exclusivity, or executable entry backing. The failure uses `InvalidStack` reason `MinimumHeadroomTooLarge` with the submitted minimum and selected status 32; the active constructor enforces and tests this fit check.
- **Stack-validation error family (selected option B).** Malformed or out-of-user-range extents, stack alignment violations, zero or oversized minimum headroom, out-of-range Call-time SP, and insufficient downward headroom use one shared `InvalidStack` error family with a typed diagnostic reason and offending value. These are recoverable pre-commit failures: construction preserves destination-slot state and authority, and Call preserves the source context and invocation stack before any push or switch. Status 32, the reason catalogue/IDs 1–12 (0 invalid) and offending-value rules are selected below. Stack-predicate precedence and admission-stage placement are specified below. Shared status/reasons, constructor validation and the independent numeric SP helper are implemented; Call-stage admission and context transitions remain deferred. Zero function addresses still use `InvalidPointer`, capability/authority failures retain their existing errors, depth exhaustion uses the separately selected `NestingDepth`, and Return underflow or a retired source AddressSpace remains fault delivery with no pop.
- **Stack-validation detail encoding (selected option A).** `InvalidStack` uses `x1` for the offending `u64` value and `x2` for one field-specific typed reason identifying both the failure and field. There is no separate operand index and no reason/operand bit packing. Base, end, minimum-headroom and SP alignment failures therefore have distinct reasons. Status 32, the reason catalogue/IDs 1–12 (0 invalid), per-reason offending values, stack-predicate order and admission-stage placement are selected below. The shared ABI, constructor validator and independent numeric SP helper are implemented and tested; Call dispatch remains unimplemented.
- **No kernel stack switch on PPC entry.** The kernel does not provide or switch the target's user stack. The target component/export setup selects a target stack extent when constructing the `Invocation`, so it can account for its concurrency and stack-pool policy. `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation` carries the target-chosen extent in its schema, and the Invocation payload stores it. The extent must be nonempty and wholly within the target AddressSpace's user virtual-address range; malformed or privileged-range extents are rejected explicitly. The extent is encoded as base and exclusive end in `x5` and `x6`, with `x7` carrying positive minimum downward headroom in bytes; the boundaries and minimum must each be multiples of 16 bytes, with no page-alignment requirement. Construction rejects `M > end - base` before installation with `InvalidStack` reason `MinimumHeadroomTooLarge`, reporting `M` with selected status 32. At `Call`, require 16-byte-aligned `SP`, `base < SP <= end`, and `SP - base >= M` for the Invocation's target-published positive minimum downward headroom. An invalid SP is rejected explicitly with `InvalidStack` before the invocation-stack push or context switch, preserving the source context and reporting the submitted SP with selected status 32 and the applicable reason. This is range validation only: the kernel does not walk page tables or prove that the whole extent is mapped writable; the target's stack setup must establish that. The Call ABI must transport this SP separately from function arguments: target-entry `x0` and `x1` are the two leading dummy arguments, while the six real inputs remain unchanged in `x2..x7`; none of these argument positions carries stack metadata. **Provisional SP-handoff selection (option A, pending end-to-end confirmation):** the Call wrapper supplies the target entry SP in `x9`, leaving `x2..x7` available as the six Call inputs. Use this working transport for the PPC trial, but do not freeze `x9` as the final ABI until end-to-end Call/Return confirms its feasibility. The current `vectors.S` saves `x8/x9` into the exception frame before calling Rust and restores them from that frame; PPC dispatch must read `frame.gpr[9]` from the saved frame, not rely on the live `x9` surviving Rust handler code. The provisional transport still requires a PPC-specific userspace wrapper and kernel dispatch path, explicit capture of the source `SP_EL0`, and installation of the target `SP_EL0` for exception return. Integer wrapper declarations cover x0..x17 plus caller-volatile x18 as selected above; the current slice prohibits/traps FP/SIMD use. End-to-end validation must demonstrate that the submitted `x9` survives SVC entry in the saved frame, the six function arguments remain in place, and Call/Return installs/restores the correct execution SP. No implementation or feasibility validation is claimed; the `InvalidStack` status is 32. Kernel PPC entry is: capability lookup → validate target stack SP → push continuation → switch translation context and keytable → redirect the exception frame.
- **Return path (current-relative Thread form).** Return is `Thread.Return` operation `0`, invoked on a fixed kernel-installed Thread capability with the explicit `CurrentReturnOnly` selector, never `Named(ObjectId)`. It names no function, AddressSpace or concrete Thread and carries no Thread-management rights. Ordinary named Thread entries do not authorize Return; the current-relative form rejects Grant/Suspend/Resume/Retire and object-identity extraction. `Invocation` remains Call-only. Invoking Return pops only the invoking Thread's own top continuation and restores source AddressSpace, SP, PC, raw SPSR/origin and x19–x30, delivering `x0 = SUCCESS (0)`, `x1 = r0`, `x2 = r1` and clearing x3..x18 under the selected scrub rules. Inputs are the target-table-local packed Slot(1) key in x0, operation `0` in x1 and payload r0/r1 in x2/x3; x4..x7 are ignored. The common native target-body return convention remains experimental. `KeySlot::THREAD_RETURN` stays Slot(1); all other slots and kind IDs (`Thread = 3`, `Invocation = 7`) remain unchanged. The sentinel is not per-invocation authority: validation state is the kernel-owned per-Thread invocation stack, following Composite's cap-0 sentinel and NOVA's implicit reply-slot precedents. Any Thread in one AddressSpace may invoke the same AS-shared sentinel, but it cannot pop another Thread's stack. Return abandons target execution and consumes exactly the immediate-source top record, never twice or out of order; frame skipping is not a kernel-detectable condition and no per-invocation token is required. This representation selection does not implement a pop, switch or success dispatch.
- **Return-key entry representation, construction, and presence.** The Thread capability selector explicitly distinguishes `Named(ObjectId)` from `CurrentReturnOnly`; the latter has no concrete object identity to extract or resolve. Only the kernel constructs the current-relative sentinel, and Kickstart installs it in the boot table at `KeySlot::THREAD_RETURN` Slot(1). Invocation payloads instead carry a mandatory `NonZero<u64>` function address, not `Option<NonZero<u64>>`; `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation` still rejects zero with `InvalidPointer` and constructs only Call authority. Presence is libOS composition policy, not a kernel guarantee. Return performs ordinary checked lookup through the invoking Thread's current AddressSpace-associated keytable, including SELF/table validation, guard, incarnation, bounds and presence; no magic raw key, guessed incarnation, slot-only fallback or lookup bypass exists. An empty Slot(1) fails as ordinary empty-slot key access. Current-relative Return-form propagation through KeyTable management remains deferred and is independent of Call-only Invocation distribution; neither an Invocation allowlist addition nor arbitrary named-Thread derivation/transfer is approved here. Remaining per-kind distribution/rights/badge restrictions stay D4 work.
- **Return validation.** After ordinary key lookup and admission of `Thread.Return` on `CurrentReturnOnly`, depth-zero underflow is the "illegal return" fault; a saved source AddressSpace in the top record that is no longer live is the "return target retired" fault. These are faults delivered to the invoking Thread's fault handler, not ordinary recoverable errors: without a valid saved continuation the Thread cannot coherently continue after Return. Underflow is a protocol violation structurally like an illegal instruction; a retired saved source is an external invalidation the Thread did not cause, and retirement can invalidate its continuation without its cooperation, leaving the callee no normal exit path. Neither case pops the invocation stack; Thread teardown releases the records. Return on a valid named Thread entry is a recoverable `InvalidOperation`; Grant/Suspend/Resume/Retire on a valid `CurrentReturnOnly` entry are likewise `InvalidOperation`, with no object-identity extraction or management authority. Empty/stale/wrong-guard keys retain ordinary lookup failures. `x1 = 0` on an ordinary Invocation is `Invocation.Call`, not a wrong-form Return: operation meaning follows the looked-up kind. Invocation operation `1` is unknown and must return `InvalidOperation` when the Call handler is enabled; the Invocation kind currently remains unsupported, so no active-handler error is claimed now. No compatibility alias or old Return opcode is retained. This fixes classification only; handler binding, fault vector and resume-with-edited-state semantics remain D1 decisions, and no fault delivery or Return success is implemented. Composite's poison-frame crash on underflow is not adopted.

**Selected `InvalidStack` reason catalogue and offending values (option A with split extent ordering):**

`x2` is the complete numeric reason ID below; `x1` reports the submitted value, not a computed difference. IDs 1–12 are selected; **0 is not a valid reason**. The `InvalidStack` status ID in `x0` is **32** (`INVALID_STACK`). Predicate precedence is specified separately below; numeric IDs do not define it. The shared status/reasons and constructor dispatch are implemented and tested. SP reasons are exercised through the independent numeric helper, not live Call dispatch.

| ID in `x2` | Reason | Condition | Offending value in `x1` |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ExtentEmpty` | `end == base` | `end` |
| 2 | `ExtentInverted` | `end < base` | `end` |
| 3 | `BaseOutsideUserRange` | Base outside the target user VA range | `base` |
| 4 | `EndOutsideUserRange` | Exclusive end exceeds the permitted user extent | `end` |
| 5 | `BaseMisaligned` | Base not 16-byte aligned | `base` |
| 6 | `EndMisaligned` | End not 16-byte aligned | `end` |
| 7 | `MinimumHeadroomZero` | `M == 0` | `M` |
| 8 | `MinimumHeadroomMisaligned` | `M` not 16-byte aligned | `M` |
| 9 | `MinimumHeadroomTooLarge` | For an ordered extent, `M > end - base` | `M` |
| 10 | `SpMisaligned` | SP not 16-byte aligned | `SP` |
| 11 | `SpOutOfRange` | `SP <= base` or `SP > end` | `SP` |
| 12 | `SpInsufficientHeadroom` | In-range SP, but `SP - base < M` | `SP` |

The exclusive end may equal the target user range's exclusive upper boundary; it need not name an accessible byte. Empty and inverted extents have distinct reasons; lower/upper SP-bound violations share `SpOutOfRange`. Relational failures report the submitted `end`, `M` or `SP`, not extent length, available headroom or deficit. Constructor and numeric SP helper tests pin these diagnostic semantics; they do not validate mappings or enable PPC execution.

**Selected stack-predicate validation order (option A — extent structure first):**

Within stack validation, stop at the first failure in the following order. This paragraph defines precedence among stack predicates; the separately selected admission-stage order below places this stage among the operation's other checks.

For `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation`:

1. Extent ordering: `ExtentEmpty` when `end == base`, otherwise `ExtentInverted` when `end < base`.
2. User-range boundaries: `BaseOutsideUserRange`, then `EndOutsideUserRange`.
3. Boundary alignment: `BaseMisaligned`, then `EndMisaligned`.
4. `MinimumHeadroomZero`.
5. `MinimumHeadroomMisaligned`.
6. `MinimumHeadroomTooLarge`, computing `end - base` only after extent ordering is valid.

For `Invocation.Call`:

1. `SpMisaligned`.
2. `SpOutOfRange`.
3. `SpInsufficientHeadroom`, computing `SP - base` only after SP is in range.

For example, `base == end == 0x1001` reports `ExtentEmpty`, not `BaseMisaligned`. A simultaneously misaligned/out-of-range SP reports `SpMisaligned`. Every rejection still precedes installation or continuation push/context switch and preserves the previously selected state/authority guarantees. Constructor and independent numeric SP helper tests cover this predicate precedence, including multiply-invalid values. Call admission/push/switch tests remain deferred.

**Selected admission-stage order (option B — supplied values before remaining admission checks):**

Capability/authority and target-identity validation precede supplied-value diagnostics. Once those checks succeed, malformed supplied values take precedence over remaining destination/resource-readiness failures. Stop at the first failed stage; all stages before commitment are state- and authority-preserving.

For `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation`:

1. Validate the operation and required capabilities, their types/authority (`GRANT` on the invoked AddressSpace, `INSTALL` on the destination KeyTable), and the live target AddressSpace identity.
2. Reject a zero function address with `InvalidPointer`.
3. Run the selected ordered extent/minimum-headroom checks.
4. Check destination-slot bounds, vacancy and installability.
5. Install the capability only after every check passes.

For `Invocation.Call`:

1. Validate the operation, Invocation key, Call-only form, applicable authority and live target AddressSpace identity.
2. Run the selected ordered SP checks.
3. Validate/prepare the target translation context (root/ASID readiness and encodability).
4. Check invocation-depth capacity.
5. Push the continuation and commit the migration only after every check passes; hardware installation remains after object/Access guards and incompatible locks end.

Thus missing authority or a stale target takes precedence over malformed supplied values. An invalid extent plus an occupied destination reports `InvalidStack`; an invalid SP plus an unready translation context or exhausted depth reports `InvalidStack`. Translation-preparation failure takes precedence over depth exhaustion once SP is valid. The function-address check still performs no mapping/executable-entry validation. This order does not select new authority bits, target-preparation error meanings or lifecycle/fault policy. The active constructor implements this admission order, with real-SVC authority/stale-target, function/extent and extent/destination priority and failure-atomicity tests. The independent numeric SP helper tests predicate precedence only: invalid-SP versus translation/depth and translation versus depth tests still require Call dispatch, which remains unimplemented.

The immediate PPC ABI decision inventory is resolved: non-payload GPR/NZCV scrubbing is selected above, with implementation/validation pending. Target-entry SPSR controls deliberately inherit the saved source status except for cleared NZCV, with implementation/validation pending. Separate architectural details remain open, including TLS/debug/other state and complete architectural-state isolation; broader authority, memory/transfer and fault/lifecycle decisions are not resolved by this inventory. FP/SIMD is prohibited/trapped for the current integer-only slice; trap enforcement and validation remain pending. x18 is selected as caller-volatile scratch with discarded clobbers in both wrappers, without record growth. Conservative x0..x17 raw-SVC compiler declarations are selected above; implementation/validation remain pending. Common export entry/linkage uses the selected per-procedure non-returning wrapper; implementation/validation remain pending. Source x19–x30 preservation is kernel-owned in each continuation record as selected above; implementation/validation remain pending. Original target-result retention uses the selected 16-byte adapter-owned target-stack spill; implementation/validation remain pending. Unexpected local SUCCESS handling is selected above as `UnexpectedReturn` status 34, not implemented. The handler's five-word extern-C calling convention is selected above. The low-level Return helper exposes Result<Infallible, CapError> as selected above. Return ignores x4..x7 as selected above. Source completion registers are selected above. The common native target-body return convention is experimental, pending end-to-end confirmation before freeze. The separate Call-time target-SP transport is provisionally selected as `x9`, pending end-to-end feasibility confirmation before ABI freeze.

### Completion and scheduling boundary

An object transition can complete now, block, or request a handoff. Blocking is not an ordinary successful return followed by an ad hoc scheduler call. Save invocation/continuation state and eventual return values explicitly, and complete the userspace return only when the operation completes or is cancelled.

Scheduling and context switching occur after relevant object references and incompatible lock guards end. A fast direct switch is an optimization of that contract, not a bypass. PPC context and continuation resources must be bounded and accounted. Any time donation associated with a PPC follows the Time accounting contract.

### Aborted-work vocabulary

Maintainer-adopted terminology; these are semantic outcome categories, not new wire statuses or an approved result layout:

| Outcome | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Rejected before admission | This attempt did not start |
| Cancelled before commit | The operation guarantees its defined commit did not occur; this does not erase all possible preparatory effects |
| Completed | The outcome is known, including an operation-specific failure or explicitly reported partial result |
| Outcome unknown | Work may have committed, but the observer cannot establish completion |

Authority validity and operation outcome are separate: revoking authority does not prove that already-delivered work did not commit. A timeout or lost completion alone cannot be reported as cancellation-before-commit. The nucleus provides local invocation, cancellation, and completion mechanisms only. Distributed/remote protocols, retries, deduplication, durable receipts, and network failure policy belong in userspace services, not the kernel. D7/D9 must specify which categories each local operation can produce and their exact ABI representation.

## Time and userspace scheduling

Time is a first-class capability to a bounded CPU budget, not just a timer object. Userspace schedulers implement policy, distribute budget hierarchically, and observe DCBs. The nucleus enforces budget consumption, deadlines, preemption, and authorized transitions.

- **Donate** authorizes execution of a target using a defined budget and can suspend the donor until yield, exhaustion, cancellation, or another specified completion.
- **Split** creates a child budget in an explicit destination, reducing the parent's remaining amount only on commit.
- **Merge** combines compatible budgets without double counting; parent/provenance and deadline compatibility are checked.
- **Query** observes remaining budget through the same checked result convention as other operations.
- Expiry/revocation prevents further execution on retired budget and produces an explicit donor/parent continuation outcome. Reclamation accounts for already-consumed time.

The research intended unused time to return to the parent scheduler. Settle whether donation is a temporary loan or permanent transfer, where the remainder resides, and what deletion/yield means before exposing consuming Rust wrappers (D8). Memory-backed storage for Time does not mint positive budget; root budget issuance/replenishment requires explicit scheduler authority and conservation rules.

Time sketches use microseconds; DCB accounting uses nanoseconds. D8 must select wire/internal units, clock and rounding/overflow behavior. Nanosecond internal accounting is a recommendation, not an unannounced ABI change. Multiprocessor ownership and simultaneous donation must not permit spending the same budget twice.

Update this table as complete slices land. Module inclusion and dispatch determine reachability, not file presence. Maintainer clarification: operation families marked excluded below are excluded because they do not compile as-is today; they remain intended functionality and must inform design and future implementation. Exclusion is not rejection of their design intent or permission to discard them.

PPC Return helper/wrapper remains unimplemented; its selected result/clobber/error contract is unchanged | `Retire` active under `RETIRE`; current-Thread self-retirement rejected, pending records cancelled/released, queued wakeup purged, pool slot reclaimed. Grant/Suspend/Resume return defined errors. `Return` `0` is selected only for `CurrentReturnOnly` but dispatch remains unimplemented: no pop, handoff or fake success | Pool-backed named Threads retain checked identity and lifecycle. The separate current-relative `CurrentReturnOnly` sentinel has no concrete Thread/AS/function target or management rights; selector construction and bootstrap installation are implemented, with management rejection independent of rights. No invocation stack is implemented |
| AddressSpace | `Activate`/`Retire` wrappers and six-input `CreateInvocation` construction wrapper active; x5/x6/x7 carry base/exclusive end/positive byte headroom | `Activate`/`Retire` active with checked authority and transitions; `CreateInvocation` validates the target identity, nonzero function and ordered extent/headroom contract before destination admission, then atomically installs a CALL-only entry carrying the immutable validated extent | Included: boot-carved `AArch64AddressSpace` objects (immutable provisioned KeyTable binding, translation root, bound ASID) behind the `AddressSpaceObject` trait; `PageTable.Map` root, `Frame.Map`, and `ASIDPool.Assign` target the AddressSpace kind |
| KeyTable | Packed selectors and destination-local results; `transfer` wraps active Move and returns the destination-local issued key | CopyDerive/Move/Delete active with guard-aware resolution: the entry resolves the invoked and destination tables through the caller's `CallerTable` context, selectors validate the table guard, and installs mint destination-guard keys; the self-table guard comes from `SELF_KEYTABLE` | Variable-size carved objects (header-authoritative capacity, `size_bits` 1–20 at Retype, carve sized from the exponent); `{address, guard, size_bits}` capability payload copied verbatim by derivation; guard validation (`GuardMismatch`) after zero-incarnation; slot counters, checked lookup/removal, atomic insertion and exhaustion tested; object lifetime/management operations unfinished |
| Frame/architecture | Frame kind encodable through the active Retype wrapper; `FrameKey` Map/Unmap/GetExtent and `PageTableKey` Map/Unmap wrappers active; `ASIDPoolKey` Assign wrapper active | Frame and PageTable creatable and invocable through the active Retype path: architecture-validated sizes, kernel-sanitized carve, real descriptor installation/teardown, alias-policy enforcement, `EXECUTE` right (kernel-privilege RW+X or RO+X); `ASIDPoolKey` Assign wrapper active; `AddressSpace.Activate` installs the bound root into `TTBR0_EL1` | Inline frame payload with full mapping identity; pooled page-table metadata; boot-provided ASID pool with bitmap allocation and per-AddressSpace bound ASID; TTBR0 page/block leaves are non-global, new descriptors are published before walks, and unmap performs correctly encoded/ordered ASID-scoped TLB invalidation observed by bidirectional live remaps; the trusted two-Thread Bounce fixture switches checked independently provisioned roots/ASIDs after guards end, with warmed same-VA/distinct-backing observations |

| Time | Excluded sketch | Excluded sketch | Excluded sketch |
| Invocation | Construction through `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation` active; `Call` wrapper unimplemented. Return belongs to Thread, not Invocation; its helper/wrapper is unimplemented | Invocation kind/Call dispatch unsupported; `CreateInvocation` installs CALL-only capabilities with immutable validated target stack extent/headroom. Only Call `0` is contracted; unknown op `1` is `InvalidOperation` once the Call handler is enabled | Mandatory `NonZero<u64>` function addresses are implemented; no optional entry or Invocation Return form exists. Target extent storage and independently tested numeric SP validation are implemented; PPC migration remains unimplemented |
| EventCount | `EventCountKey` advance/await_ge/read active with the shared decoder and `WAIT_INFINITE`; the `EventCountReader` helper is active; errors preserved, including `CounterOverflow` | `Advance`/`Await`/`Read` active through real SVC dispatch: `SEND`/`RECV` authority, nonzero-delta requirement, checked arithmetic, overflow rejection with the shared status, broadcast wakeups completing every satisfied `Await` in queue order with the new value, overflow error-completing every queued `Await`, already-satisfied awaits, finite timeouts rejected until the time subsystem | Retype-creatable from the bootstrap-carved event-count pool (no Untyped bytes); object state core active: monotonic counter, bounded await queue, teardown cancellation; the completed pending-invocation record carries the full result shape (status + two words), so error wakeups resume with their status; thread-teardown-driven cancellation of a torn-down waiter's records is implemented |
| Notification | `NotificationKey` signal/wait/poll active with the shared decoder and `WAIT_INFINITE`; errors preserved | `Signal`/`Poll`/`Wait` active through real SVC dispatch: `SEND`/`RECV` authority, badge-hybrid bits, strict zero-argument checks; `Wait`'s already-satisfied path consumes pending bits and a would-block wait parks the caller — the entry copies execution state into the Thread, selects through the bounded runnable-thread fixture scheduler, and resume rewrites the active transient frame with the completed bitmap (validated end-to-end by the debug-gated Bounce fixture in `just test-capability-boot`); finite timeouts rejected until the time subsystem | Retype-creatable from the bootstrap-carved notification pool (no Untyped bytes); object state core active: coalescing bitmap, bounded wait queue, one-consumer delivery, teardown cancellation, pending-invocation records with enforced single terminal transitions; parked-thread contexts and the runnable queue live in the Thread pool and scheduler; thread-teardown-driven cancellation of a torn-down waiter's records and queued wakeups is implemented (`Nucleus::cancel_thread_pending`, validated by the boot fixture) |

KeyTable clients now encode complete keys for the invoked table, source selector and destination-table capability. CopyDerive/grant return the destination-local RawKey. `grant_to` still requests the prototype `Rights::all()` and Revoke remains an excluded legacy request, not an approved revocation mechanism. `transfer` encodes active Move and preserves kernel errors and the destination-local issued key. CopyDerive/Move/Delete use checked table authority and guarded resolution; the Bounce retained-mapping fixture exercises CopyDerive without implicit Map and Move without losing mapping records. Selective Revoke and general reclaimable storage remain deferred. Thread public handle construction remains deferred because its observation methods do not yet establish DCB mapping/lifetime safety.

The nucleus is inert at boot: Kickstart builds the initial `Nucleus` in memory carved from a boot Untyped's unused watermark range, installs the boot Thread, its AddressSpace, and initial grants (boot Untyped at `KeySlot::BOOT_UNTYPED`, the boot ASID pool at `KeySlot::BOOT_ASID_POOL`, debug console at `KeySlot::DEBUG_CONSOLE`), and records the carved address via the exported `nucleus_set_anchor` setter; the nucleus reads that anchor under a separate kernel lock on each syscall. There is no lazy `NUCLEUS` fixture, `BOOT_DOMAIN_STORAGE`, or in-nucleus bootstrap bridge. No SVC bootstrap operation, slot-to-key refresh, or guessed incarnation is introduced. The nucleus object model is exposed as a lib so Kickstart can construct the identical `Nucleus` value. This is boot-ceremony carving, not implementation of general Untyped-backed pools, kernel-private KeyTable backing, or hostile-EL0 isolation. `just test-capability-boot` runs QEMU directly: in-guest assertions validate actual key handoff, successful debug Write through SVC, and exact zero-incarnation rejection. Semihosting exits with success after boot completes or failure on panic; no host log parser or recipe-level timeout is used.

Core-ID drift is now reconciled through the shared catalogues, with exhaustive raw/index conversion tests. Remaining cross-layer blockers include unmigrated excluded clients, unsafe general pointer-derived references, unchecked exception origins/user memory, scheduler-record layout/reuse drift, independent object lifetime metadata, incomplete rights, and deferred completion beyond the active two-word `Notification.Wait`/`EventCount.Await` park/resume paths. PPC `Invocation` and its context/return ABI remain unimplemented. The guarded key-space package (per-table userspace-chosen guards, variable-size KeyTables, `GuardMismatch`, and the `SELF_KEYTABLE`-sourced self-table guard) is implemented and validated. Slot-counter safety is implemented, not general object-generation/reclamation safety. They are tracked as work in the plan, not adopted as intended behavior.

There are no validated performance promises here. Measure supported paths after correctness and state what target/workload was measured.

## Decision register

Resolve the decisions needed by a slice before enabling it. An unrelated open decision need not block pure ABI tests or repairs to the active path. Record the chosen contract here, its rationale and compatibility impact, and then update the checklist. Do not infer approval from a suggested default.

| ID | Open decision and constraints | Needed before |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Selected: hostile native code; explicit capability-scoped trust; AddressSpace = protection boundary (the core Thread executes in an arch AddressSpace); userspace-only services initially; memory confidentiality/integrity; shared numerical meaning/cheap fbuf sharing with separate protected-context fallback; no intra-AddressSpace frame aliases, cross-AddressSpace aliases/writers allowed; DMA mediation/IOMMU; libOS availability policy; side channels deferred; authoritative revocation over borrows. Selected follow-up: fbuf addresses agreed before mapping; multi-node global allocation out of scope; stale raw-pointer prevention after VA reuse delegated outside the kernel for now. Open: machine-local namespace/reservation conflicts, per-target protection features, fault handling and Rust reference contracts. Far-future: stronger temporal-VA guarantees. | Thread/AddressSpace protection and mapping semantics |
| D2 | Selected: explicit management authority carries bookkeeping responsibility and composition-scoped TCB membership; multiple/hierarchical userspace managers are policy; no mandatory central post-hoc registration. SPeCK-like kernel mechanisms retire objects and reject old invocations while manager cleanup can run in background; libOS orchestrates unmap before invalidation. Open: checked management-operation schemas, selective subtree invalidation of a still-live object, completion/handshake, and safe reuse enforcement. | General derivation/revoke and reclaiming retyped memory |
| D3 | Selected: caller-local slot/incarnation keys with implicit table context; initial 32-bit table-relative address plus 32-bit incarnation (an optional type tag would take 8 guard-field bits); fixed capacity initially (superseded 2026-09-23 by the Retype-selected `size_bits` capacity), runtime resizing out of scope; independent object identity; slot-local exhaustion without wrap and with cleanup permitted; derive rather than mutate authority in place initially; Move yields a destination-local key; inconsistency with diagnostic reasons; enforced single-core guarded access, SMP deferred; Untyped-backed kernel-private KeyTables; no automatic leak recovery; correctness-first layouts; intended private MappedSlice cap. Selected follow-up: packed incarnation-high/slot-low 64-bit key, no initial type tag, increment-on-install from 1, retained counters on deletion, no live-Thread logical table rebinding, non-owning typed handles, statuses 26–28 with diagnostic reasons, and 64-bit kernel allocation generations without wrap. Concrete guarded access selected: entries naming pooled objects store pool tag + index + generation only (no raw pointers); `CurrentReturnOnly` is explicitly not a named object and rejects object_id extraction while retaining ordinary checked key lookup; per-pool authoritative slot metadata validated before dereference, per-invocation `!Send` access context with borrow-checked guard lifetimes, and explicit alias-rejecting pair resolution. Untyped-backed carve primitives and the boot Untyped allocation are established in Kickstart; KeyTable backing and full pool/metadata ownership semantics remain Phase 5. Selected follow-up (2026-09-23): the key's low 32 bits are a table-relative address — a userspace-chosen per-table guard above the `size_bits`-wide slot index — with variable-size KeyTables (`size_bits` 1–20 at Retype), the guard stored in the kernel-visible KeyTable capability payload and copied verbatim on derivation, minted keys carrying the installing table's guard, the self-table guard sourced from `SELF_KEYTABLE` (absent/mismatched self-capability is a defined rejection), destination slot arguments as bare indices, and `GuardMismatch` as InvalidKey reason 4. Open: thread lifecycle integration, future in-place authority-mutation rules, Untyped-backed pool/metadata ownership (Phase 5), unsafe full-borrow/fbuf protocols, per-kind details, and interim Frame slot no-reuse scope. Stronger stale-raw-pointer protection across VA reuse is deferred. | General capability access, thread teardown, safe client ownership APIs |
| D4 | Selected: Retype origin caps carry delegable lifetime-control permission; donation preserves the creator's retained control; KeyTable management authority, not a special manager identity, enables direct derivation with entrusted bookkeeping. Separately grantable table permissions and incarnation-checked management operands follow the general authorization rule without manager-identity exceptions; minimal CopyDerive/Move/Delete schemas, vacant destinations and same-slot Move rejection are selected for KeyTable/debug-gated DebugConsole. Thread lifecycle control is `RETIRE` (bit `0x20`): `Thread.Retire` requires it on the invoked named Thread capability; `CurrentReturnOnly` carries no Thread-management rights and rejects Retire; delegable like other permissions, no privileged owner identity (`Rights::all()` is `0x3F`). Kickstart establishes initial Untypeds/KeyTables and delegates onward. DebugConsole remains opt-in `debug_kernel`, not generally available. The inert-nucleus handoff is selected: Kickstart builds the initial `Nucleus` in carved memory and records it via `nucleus_set_anchor`; the nucleus reads the anchor under a separate kernel lock. Open: per-operation rights/bit assignments and origin-only remap enforcement, badges, independent current-relative Return-form propagation and Call-only Invocation distribution restrictions (no arbitrary named-Thread allowlist approval), exact bootstrap Threads/AddressSpaces/capacities/placement/grants and incarnation-bearing handoff records, notification indexing, Thread.Grant relationship to KeyTable operations, and ownership of well-known `KeySlot` constants between shared API and bootstrap/libOS layout. | Exposing those authorities or bootstrap records |
| D5 | Selected: remove `DcbPages`/`DcbView` and the publicly visible global DCB view. Scheduler-owned pages are explicitly shared to the kernel through `Scheduler.ShareRegion`; the Frame must already be mapped into the scheduler's AddressSpace, one Frame per call, and conflicting re-share/replacement is rejected. `Scheduler` marks a Thread authorized to schedule; derive it for hierarchical schedulers. Kickstart establishes the root scheduler before scheduling starts; only the root creates Threads, then may donate them to subordinate schedulers, with strict-tree enforcement required. Scheduler-writable shared records are separate from kernel-private TCB/execution state; begin with the existing DcbPage/TCB division, exact field split TBD. Kernel lookup uses a scheduler-declared fixed-stride table keyed by validated Thread identity/incarnation. Core grouping is `Thread = 3`, `Time = 4`, `Scheduler = 5`, `Brand = 6`, `Invocation = 7`, `Notification = 8`, `EventCount = 9`. Brand targets IPI/thread-handler upcalls, with exact binding/IRQ relation open and implementation deferred to IRQ work. Open: exact ShareRegion register schema/rights, scheduler-table declaration/capacity/allocation, record field semantics and synchronization, thread creation/donation and strict-tree enforcement, event summaries, teardown/reuse/reclamation. | Scheduler-shared record ABI and Thread lifecycle integration |
| D6 | Selected Frame direction: Copy is not Map; each copied cap can Map the same frame at a different AddressSpace/virtual page; Unmap is local and origin cap-Revoke withdraws descendants; origin remap remains a valid operation; full revoke/no Frame-slot reuse on deprovision is interim. Selected Retype invariant: only Untyped unused-watermark allocations with no outstanding access, not arbitrary live-object conversion or an allocation-time unmap protocol. Selected Frame-carve sanitization: the kernel zeroes Retype-carved frame contents inside the transaction, before capability installation and watermark commit; device sources are rejected earlier. Selected mapping-context identity: the AddressSpace (arch index 2) is the mapping context. Selected ASID model: explicit capability-protected ASIDPool resources with authorized AssignASID-style binding to an AddressSpace's translation root; the reserved ASIDControl kind (index 4) is the namespace's control point, and ASID release goes through `AddressSpace.Retire` (whole-ASID invalidation, then release to the originating pool). Selected execute right: `EXECUTE` (bit 0x10) is a real frame right; a `Frame.Map` requested with it (within the ceiling) clears UXN|PXN — with `WRITE` as kernel-privilege RW+X (AP=00, EL0 denied, since EL1 cannot execute EL0-writable pages), without `WRITE` as read-only executable at EL0 and EL1. Selected activation: `AddressSpace.Activate` installs the bound root into the current hardware translation context (`MAP` authority, root+ASID required, current-caller-only; installation follows checked preparation after guards end); full Thread Start/Suspend/Resume remains Phase 7. Selected Buffer role: Buffer is a userspace/libOS construct over frame capabilities; no kernel Buffer object, handler, pool, or wire kind. The core catalogue groups `Thread = 3`, `Time = 4`, `Scheduler = 5`, `Brand = 6`, `Invocation = 7`, `Notification = 8`, and `EventCount = 9`; `DebugConsole` keeps 127. Open: shared-namespace scope/conflicts and alias enforcement, precise Map/remap/revoke/no-reuse schemas and orchestration, three sharing-mode/fbuf protocols, Retype layout/accounting and enforcement of unused-watermark/no-outstanding-access allocation, broader sanitization duties beyond Retype-carved Frames, complete per-mapping record contents, ASID binding/reuse schemas and hardware-safe reuse, whether ASID identity belongs in high `VirtAddr` bits, and device rules. Stale raw-pointer temporal-VA protection is far-future work. | Memory-object vertical slice |
| D7 | Adopted aborted-work vocabulary and local-mechanisms-only kernel boundary. Selected: `Invocation` is a Protected Procedure Call — it identifies a component API entry point in a target AddressSpace, and the source Thread migrates into that AddressSpace; userspace may compose queued rendezvous over Invocation plus Notification/EventCount. Selected execution/return directions follow; call lifecycle, cancellation, and teardown semantics remain open. Selected wait semantics for Notification/EventCount: pending wait identity is a non-wrapping 64-bit-generation record; one relative `u64`-nanosecond timeout (`u64::MAX` infinite, zero invalid, no-wait via Poll/nonblocking variants, no absolute deadlines initially); timeout and Thread teardown cancellation; one-consumer Notification and broadcast EventCount; terminal transition is exactly once. Selected event-count overflow: nonzero delta; overflow returns `CounterOverflow` 31 without changing the counter and error-completes queued Await operations. Selected shared-payload ordering: kernel-mediated release/acquire; DMA/device completion excluded. Selected PPC execution directions (2026-09-28 onward): interrupt-kernel discipline (per-core kernel stack; a Thread's continuation never lives on a kernel stack; the parked-frame SVC path is not the PPC mechanism); a Thread-resident bounded invocation stack of kernel-owned continuation records (source AddressSpace identity, saved user SP/PC, return metadata), with the top stored in the Thread and call-time depth-exhaustion rejection; no kernel stack switch on entry — target stack extents are selected at `AddressSpace.CreateInvocation`, and the kernel validates the Call-time SP against the published extent. Selected return form: `Thread.Return` operation `0`, on a kernel-installed Thread entry with explicit `CurrentReturnOnly` selector, not `Named(ObjectId)`. It has no function, AddressSpace or concrete Thread target, no Thread-management rights, and rejects object_id extraction and Grant/Suspend/Resume/Retire. Named Thread entries reject Return with `InvalidOperation`. Any Thread in the same AddressSpace may invoke the AS-shared sentinel, but only its own current top continuation may be popped; no per-invocation return capability or reply object (Composite's cap-0 sentinel and NOVA's implicit reply-slot precedents). `KeySlot::THREAD_RETURN` stays Slot(1); self AddressSpace 2, parent Thread 3, self KeyTable 4, boot Untyped 5, boot ASID pool 14, debug console 15 and test destinations 6–13 remain unchanged, as do kind IDs Thread 3/Invocation 7. Kernel construction and Kickstart installation select the current-relative form; ordinary caller-table/SELF, guard, incarnation, bounds and presence checks are mandatory, with no magic raw key or lookup bypass. Presence remains libOS policy. Current-relative Return-form propagation is deferred independently of Call Invocation distribution; no arbitrary named-Thread allowlist or transfer approval. Invocation is Call-only with mandatory `NonZero<u64>` function address, not Option; CreateInvocation zero remains `InvalidPointer`. On an ordinary Invocation, x1=0 is Call, not wrong-form Return; unknown Invocation op 1 is `InvalidOperation` once the Call handler is enabled (kind currently unsupported). No compatibility aliases or old Return opcode. Selected Return faults remain "illegal return" (depth-zero underflow) and "return target retired" (saved source AS no longer live), with no pop; teardown releases records. Fault delivery/binding/resumption remain D1 work. Context redirection makes the target structurally single-use; no frame-skipping detector or return token. Opcode/selector/bootstrap representation is implemented; all PPC dispatch remains pending, with no Return success or fake success. Selected invocation-stack storage, record, and depth (2026-09-29): a fixed-depth array of continuation records inline in the Thread pool entry (no separate pool, no runtime allocation; thread capacity is the libOS `PoolCapacities.threads` knob), depth 16 (record layout and type-derived pool accounting verified; Call admission remains unimplemented), record = source AddressSpace `ObjectId` + source return PC + source user SP + an 8 B per-call time stamp + source SPSR + source exception origin + twelve source x19–x30 words (kernel preservation option B; copy saved frame before Call commit and restore on Return, independently of x0/x1/x2 completion; no mandatory source spill stub). The measured record is 144 B and the depth-16 record array 2304 B; type-derived Thread/pool layout and accounting are validated, without requiring the whole multi-Thread pool to fit one page. The top index lives in the Thread entry. Recoverable call-time depth exhaustion uses `CapError::NestingDepth` / `NESTING_DEPTH = 33` before push/switch (`x1` current/maximum count, `x2` zero; `(33, 16, 0)` at depth 16; shared status and storage are complete; Call admission remains unimplemented), Composite-style nesting. Terminology: a Thread's capability table is its keytable; Vesper documentation and code use this term. Selected (2026-10-02): each AddressSpace names exactly one keytable shared by its Threads; Call selects the target table from the target AddressSpace, and the source AddressSpace identity in the continuation selects the table on Return. AddressSpace provisioning and caller-table lookup now use an immutable issued binding to initialized, retained carved storage; no per-Thread table field remains. Future reclaimable table identity/backing protection remains D3 work. The trusted two-Thread Bounce wait/resume fixture now switches real independently provisioned roots/ASIDs and current-AS tables; same-Thread PPC migration remains outstanding. Selected CreateInvocation extent/headroom encoding: `x5` base, `x6` exclusive end, `x7` positive minimum headroom as a direct `u64` byte count (not an exponent); published byte extent `[base, end)`, rejecting `base >= end`. Selected Call-time SP rule: 16-byte alignment, `base < SP <= end`, and `SP - base >= M` for an agreed positive minimum downward headroom; `SP = end` is allowed, `SP = base` is not. Selected minimum-headroom source (option B): the target publishes positive `M` at CreateInvocation, the Invocation stores it, and Call enforces it; no fixed 4 KiB floor is selected. Selected stack-extent/headroom alignment (option A): `base`, `end`, and `M` are each multiples of 16 bytes, with no page-alignment requirement. Selected construction-time headroom fit (option A): reject `M > end - base` before capability installation, preserving destination-slot state and authority; check extent ordering before subtracting. Equality is valid and leaves `SP = end` as the only valid initial stack position. Selected stack-validation error family (option B): `InvalidStack` with a typed diagnostic reason and offending value for extent, alignment, minimum-headroom and Call-time SP failures; these are recoverable pre-commit rejections, not Return faults or replacements for unrelated capability/authority errors. Selected stack-validation detail encoding (option A): `x1` offending `u64` value, `x2` field-specific typed reason identifying failure and field, with no operand-index packing. Selected reason catalogue/value semantics: distinct `ExtentEmpty`/`ExtentInverted` (report `end`), base/end user-range and alignment reasons (report that boundary), zero/misaligned/oversized minimum headroom (report `M`), and SP misalignment/out-of-range/insufficient-headroom reasons (report `SP`). Lower/upper SP-bound failures share `SpOutOfRange`; relational diagnostics report submitted values, not computed differences. Selected numeric reason IDs: 1–12 in the canonical table's order (`ExtentEmpty` 1 through `SpInsufficientHeadroom` 12), with 0 invalid; these are full `x2` values and do not themselves define validation order. Selected stack-validation status: `INVALID_STACK = 32` in `x0`, with offending value in `x1` and reason ID in `x2`; the separately selected depth-exhaustion status is `NESTING_DEPTH = 33` (`x1` current/maximum count, `x2` zero), status 32/reasons 1–12 and lossless decoding are implemented, and status 33 plus inline depth-16 storage are implemented; Call admission remains unimplemented. Selected stack-predicate precedence (option A): construction checks extent ordering, base/end user range, base/end alignment, zero minimum, minimum alignment, then minimum fit; Call checks SP alignment, SP bounds, then headroom. Stop at the first failure, and validate ordering/bounds before subtraction. Selected admission-stage placement (option B): capability/authority and live target identity first; construction then checks nonzero function address and ordered stack predicates before destination-slot admission; Call checks SP before target translation preparation and depth capacity. All checks precede commitment, and malformed values take precedence over remaining destination/readiness/depth errors. The implemented six-input constructor and immutable numeric stack contract replace reserved-zero x5..x7 without a compatibility encoding. Constructor priority/failure atomicity and the independent SP helper are tested; live Call-stage priority is not. Inline extent storage grows `KeyEntry` to 64 B/alignment 32 with a 40 B payload union; the unchanged 32 B header plus 256 entries/counters carves 17,440 B. Type-derived backing/accounting and layout tests cover the growth. Selected AArch64 target-entry arguments: two leading dummy arguments in `x0`/`x1`, with the six real `u64` inputs unchanged in `x2..x7` and no register shuffle; stack metadata uses the separate SP transport. Provisionally selected SP transport: the wrapper supplies target entry SP in `x9`, and kernel dispatch reads saved `frame.gpr[9]`, not live x9 after Rust entry. This is the working trial ABI, not frozen until end-to-end Call/Return confirms feasibility; conservative x0..x17 raw-SVC compiler declarations are selected (Call x0..x2 input/results, x3..x7 and x9 inputs/discarded outputs, x8/x10..x17 clobbered; local Return x0..x2 input/responses, x3 input/discarded output and x4..x17 clobbered). No extra result words/x3..x17 record fields or ignored-operand initialization; memory/flags conservative, nostack initially omitted and fallible Return not noreturn. x18 is selected as caller-volatile scratch and a discarded clobber in both wrappers, without extra record fields or a reserved platform role. FP/SIMD is prohibited/trapped for the current integer-only slice (soft-float builds plus explicit effective trap controls for EL1t/EL0; execution-fault classification, D1 delivery still open; no vector/control-state storage or automatic enable). Trap/compiler/PPC validation is pending. Selected non-payload policy: successful Call retains x2..x7, zeroes x0/x1 and x8..x30 after source capture/x9 consumption, and clears target NZCV; successful Return delivers x0..x2, zeroes x3..x18, and restores source x19..x30/context/raw SPSR. No migration scrubbing on recoverable local rejection, no source-side dummy-zeroing or record growth, and no complete architectural-state isolation claim; implementation/tests remain pending. Selected target-entry execution-status controls (option B): inherit immediate source saved SPSR execution mode, interrupt masks and other non-NZCV controls, clearing only NZCV; never copy live kernel-handler status or elevate EL0 execution. Return restores exact raw source SPSR. Status implementation/validation remain pending; TLS/debug/complete architectural-state isolation remain open. Selected integer results: two target-provided u64 payload words separate from kernel status; successful source resumption is x0=SUCCESS/x1=r0/x2=r1, and `Thread.Return` SVC inputs are target-table-local packed Slot(1) return key in x0, op 0 in x1, r0/r1 in x2/x3; Return ignores x4..x7 without reserved-zero checks or an adapter clearing requirement. The low-level Return helper exposes Result<Infallible, CapError>: success leaves the target, while ordinary rejection returns Err with shared diagnostics and no pop/migration; underflow/retired saved source remain faults. The common export adapter retains original (r0,r1) in a selected 16-byte target-stack frame area before key lookup/helper calls, keeps SP 16-byte aligned, loads Return payload and error-handoff originals from that pair, and reports Err with it to a non-returning libOS fault handler for diagnosis/explicit retry/termination; export setup budgets the spill and other adapter overhead, without kernel/heap allocation or invocation-record growth. This retention selection is not implemented/validated and does not settle source preservation or final clobbers. Selected common export linkage: CreateInvocation receives a per-procedure non-returning extern-C wrapper address; the wrapper accepts two dummy plus six real u64 arguments, calls its linked body normally with real inputs in x2..x7 and target-local body linkage, then performs the selected spill/Return/error path without returning through incoming x30. No new constructor metadata, kernel-installed return trampoline or entry-signature validation; custom entries/direct Return remain possible, and wrapper implementation/validation are pending; no automatic retry or body re-execution, with a provisionally link-resolved component-image libOS entry point for this userspace path, not kernel-origin fault binding; its five-u64 extern-C non-returning ABI is selected (status/detail1/detail2/original r0/original r1 in x0..x4, error triple from CapError::code(), no Rust enum layout or diagnostic pointer); handler symbol is `vesper_thread_return_fault`. Selected unexpected local Return SUCCESS: the helper returns Err(CapError::UnexpectedReturn { word1, word2 }), retaining local x1/x2 as diagnostics; `UNEXPECTED_RETURN = 34` encodes (34, word1, word2), and the adapter passes this plus original body results to that handler. This userspace-synthesized invariant error is not proof of pre-commit rejection/unchanged state/safe retry; no fabricated Infallible/Ok, automatic trap/retry or change to ordinary status-zero decoding. Implementation/validation pending. The common extern-C target body returning a repr-C two-u64 struct in x0/x1 is experimental and remains unfrozen pending end-to-end confirmation; these conventions are not implemented/validated PPC. Six payload words are a possible later extension, not selected. Selected per-call time accounting (2026-09-29): the record's stamp brackets Call→Return and the elapsed time is attributed to the source Thread's own DCB; broader hierarchical attribution is deferred, and the arithmetic stays inert until Time exists. Selected fully interrupt-kernel blocking (2026-09-29): the blocked-wait continuation is Thread-resident, retiring the parked kernel-stack frame as a continuation carrier for waits as well as calls, with at most one parked continuation per Thread distinct from the invocation stack. Open for PPC: current-relative Return-form propagation independently of Call-only Invocation distribution; badge/delegation; TLS/debug/other state and complete architectural-state isolation beyond selected saved-source status inheritance; final confirmation of the provisional x9 SP transport after end-to-end validation; capability transfer; nested/concurrent-call rules beyond the depth bound; faults, cancellation, teardown, and bounded resource accounting. The selected stack policy is an Invocation-specific target extent plus aligned Call-time SP range checking, without a page-table walk. Function-pointer/entry validation at construction is not required. The rendezvous-specific label-plus-five-word layout, extended x1..x7 completion outputs, IPC-buffer spill, reply phase, late-reply behavior, and reply reservation are not selected for PPC. | Protected Procedure Call and wait-operation ABI |
| D8 | Budget issuance, donation loan/transfer, unused-budget return, split/merge/deletion/expiry, units/clocks, multicore accounting. | Time/scheduler vertical slice |
| D9 | DebugConsole gate preserves type `127` and Write `0`; no new inline-byte ABI is approved. The approved key package specifies packed keys, statuses 26–28, diagnostic reasons/precedence, CopyDerive register packing and coordinated rebuild with no slot-only fallback. Minimal KeyTable logical schemas are selected; Move/Delete's remaining wire details are pending. The KeyTable Retype `x3` packing (userspace-chosen guard in bits 39:8 above the `size_bits` byte; bits 63:40 reserved zero, and for every other kind bits 63:8 reserved zero) and InvalidKey reason `GuardMismatch = 4` are selected (2026-09-23); the exact status for a missing or mismatched self-table capability remains open. Aborted-work terms are adopted vocabulary only. `COUNTER_OVERFLOW` 31 (zero details) is the shared event-count overflow error. `Thread.Return` `0` is selected on `CurrentReturnOnly`, with target-table-local packed Slot(1) key in x0, x1=0, r0/r1 in x2/x3 and ignored x4..x7; Invocation is Call-only `0`, with no Return opcode/alias. Kind IDs and all slot indices stay unchanged; opcode/selector/bootstrap representation is implemented, while PPC dispatch remains unimplemented. `Thread.Retire` `4` is selected on named Thread entries: no arguments (`x2..x7` zero), zeros result, no new status — rejections reuse the existing shared errors. `AddressSpace.Activate` `0` and `AddressSpace.Retire` `1` are selected on the AddressSpace arch kind. There is no compatibility encoding for superseded numbering — coordinated rebuild. Wire encodings, other shared errors/details, reserved/unsupported operations, ABI version/support discovery, and migration policy remain open. | Freezing new operation schemas or separately deployed ABI consumers |

## Definition of a supported operation

A supported operation has one shared schema; a client that preserves results; checked nucleus decoding and authorization for every participating resource; typed state transitions with explicit ownership/failure semantics; documented blocking and teardown behavior; and validation at the appropriate ABI, model, and target-integration levels.

Safety comments describe actual invariants and their owner, not just that a call is unsafe. Layout and round-trip assertions remain enabled. Tests include malformed and adversarial requests, aliasing/identity reuse, capacity failures, cancellation, and rollback where applicable.

Follow the [implementation plan](capabilities_implementation_plan.md) in small dependency-respecting slices. The repository skill at `.agents/skills/capability-refactor/SKILL.md` describes the working procedure; it does not replace the contracts in this document.


## Canonical sections moved out of the active contract

### Sources and supersession

### Sources and supersession

This document consolidates:

- The former `kernel/nucleus/design.md`: object identity and storage, architecture associated types, typed frame sizes, VSpace composition, uniform dispatch, open/closed waits, and separate send/receive timeouts. That file is retired in favor of this reference.
- The research note **Kernel API Surface.md**, located in the Metta notes at `Vesper/API/Kernel API Surface.md`: Composite-inspired delegation and userspace resource managers; Nemesis-inspired shared-address-space direction, DCB observations, event counts, and self-scheduling; seL4-inspired untyped allocation, notifications, and capability operations.
- The cross-layer review of `kernel/nucleus/src/api/`, `kernel/nucleus/src/objects/`, `libs/object/`, and the adjacent syscall transport.

The research is inspiration, not a wire ABI. Its example numbering, object sizes, register layouts, slot allocations, standalone syscall list, Rust ownership claims, and cycle estimates are not authoritative. In particular, **the existing `CoreType` discriminants are the correct core numbering**, not the former conflicting `ObjectType` constants or the earlier review's suggested preservation of those constants.


### Implementation status and known migration gaps

## Implementation status and known migration gaps

Update this table as complete slices land. Module inclusion and dispatch determine reachability, not file presence. Maintainer clarification: operation families marked excluded below are excluded because they do not compile as-is today; they remain intended functionality and must inform design and future implementation. Exclusion is not rejection of their design intent or permission to discard them.

| Family | Userspace | Nucleus API | Object/storage |

PPC Return helper/wrapper remains unimplemented; its selected result/clobber/error contract is unchanged | `Retire` active under `RETIRE`; current-Thread self-retirement rejected, pending records cancelled/released, queued wakeup purged, pool slot reclaimed. Grant/Suspend/Resume return defined errors. `Return` `0` is selected only for `CurrentReturnOnly` but dispatch remains unimplemented: no pop, handoff or fake success | Pool-backed named Threads retain checked identity and lifecycle. The separate current-relative `CurrentReturnOnly` sentinel has no concrete Thread/AS/function target or management rights; selector construction and bootstrap installation are implemented, with management rejection independent of rights. No invocation stack is implemented |
| AddressSpace | `Activate`/`Retire` wrappers and six-input `CreateInvocation` construction wrapper active; x5/x6/x7 carry base/exclusive end/positive byte headroom | `Activate`/`Retire` active with checked authority and transitions; `CreateInvocation` validates the target identity, nonzero function and ordered extent/headroom contract before destination admission, then atomically installs a CALL-only entry carrying the immutable validated extent | Included: boot-carved `AArch64AddressSpace` objects (immutable provisioned KeyTable binding, translation root, bound ASID) behind the `AddressSpaceObject` trait; `PageTable.Map` root, `Frame.Map`, and `ASIDPool.Assign` target the AddressSpace kind |
| KeyTable | Packed selectors and destination-local results; `transfer` wraps active Move and returns the destination-local issued key | CopyDerive/Move/Delete active with guard-aware resolution: the entry resolves the invoked and destination tables through the caller's `CallerTable` context, selectors validate the table guard, and installs mint destination-guard keys; the self-table guard comes from `SELF_KEYTABLE` | Variable-size carved objects (header-authoritative capacity, `size_bits` 1–20 at Retype, carve sized from the exponent); `{address, guard, size_bits}` capability payload copied verbatim by derivation; guard validation (`GuardMismatch`) after zero-incarnation; slot counters, checked lookup/removal, atomic insertion and exhaustion tested; object lifetime/management operations unfinished |

Return belongs to Thread, not Invocation; its helper/wrapper is unimplemented | Invocation kind/Call dispatch unsupported; `CreateInvocation` installs CALL-only capabilities with immutable validated target stack extent/headroom. Only Call `0` is contracted; unknown op `1` is `InvalidOperation` once the Call handler is enabled | Mandatory `NonZero<u64>` function addresses are implemented; no optional entry or Invocation Return form exists. Target extent storage and independently tested numeric SP validation are implemented; PPC migration remains unimplemented |
| EventCount | `EventCountKey` advance/await_ge/read active with the shared decoder and `WAIT_INFINITE`; the `EventCountReader` helper is active; errors preserved, including `CounterOverflow` | `Advance`/`Await`/`Read` active through real SVC dispatch: `SEND`/`RECV` authority, nonzero-delta requirement, checked arithmetic, overflow rejection with the shared status, broadcast wakeups completing every satisfied `Await` in queue order with the new value, overflow error-completing every queued `Await`, already-satisfied awaits, finite timeouts rejected until the time subsystem | Retype-creatable from the bootstrap-carved event-count pool (no Untyped bytes); object state core active: monotonic counter, bounded await queue, teardown cancellation; the completed pending-invocation record carries the full result shape (status + two words), so error wakeups resume with their status; thread-teardown-driven cancellation of a torn-down waiter's records is implemented |

KeyTable clients now encode complete keys for the invoked table, source selector and destination-table capability. CopyDerive/grant return the destination-local RawKey. `grant_to` still requests the prototype `Rights::all()` and Revoke remains an excluded legacy request, not an approved revocation mechanism. `transfer` encodes active Move and preserves kernel errors and the destination-local issued key. CopyDerive/Move/Delete use checked table authority and guarded resolution; the Bounce retained-mapping fixture exercises CopyDerive without implicit Map and Move without losing mapping records. Selective Revoke and general reclaimable storage remain deferred. Thread public handle construction remains deferred because its observation methods do not yet establish DCB mapping/lifetime safety.

The nucleus is inert at boot: Kickstart builds the initial `Nucleus` in memory carved from a boot Untyped's unused watermark range, installs the boot Thread, its AddressSpace, and initial grants (boot Untyped at `KeySlot::BOOT_UNTYPED`, the boot ASID pool at `KeySlot::BOOT_ASID_POOL`, debug console at `KeySlot::DEBUG_CONSOLE`), and records the carved address via the exported `nucleus_set_anchor` setter; the nucleus reads that anchor under a separate kernel lock on each syscall. There is no lazy `NUCLEUS` fixture, `BOOT_DOMAIN_STORAGE`, or in-nucleus bootstrap bridge. No SVC bootstrap operation, slot-to-key refresh, or guessed incarnation is introduced. The nucleus object model is exposed as a lib so Kickstart can construct the identical `Nucleus` value. This is boot-ceremony carving, not implementation of general Untyped-backed pools, kernel-private KeyTable backing, or hostile-EL0 isolation. `just test-capability-boot` runs QEMU directly: in-guest assertions validate actual key handoff, successful debug Write through SVC, and exact zero-incarnation rejection. Semihosting exits with success after boot completes or failure on panic; no host log parser or recipe-level timeout is used.

Core-ID drift is now reconciled through the shared catalogues, with exhaustive raw/index conversion tests. Remaining cross-layer blockers include unmigrated excluded clients, unsafe general pointer-derived references, unchecked exception origins/user memory, scheduler-record layout/reuse drift, independent object lifetime metadata, incomplete rights, and deferred completion beyond the active two-word `Notification.Wait`/`EventCount.Await` park/resume paths. PPC `Invocation` and its context/return ABI remain unimplemented. The guarded key-space package (per-table userspace-chosen guards, variable-size KeyTables, `GuardMismatch`, and the `SELF_KEYTABLE`-sourced self-table guard) is implemented and validated. Slot-counter safety is implemented, not general object-generation/reclamation safety. They are tracked as work in the plan, not adopted as intended behavior.

There are no validated performance promises here. Measure supported paths after correctness and state what target/workload was measured.


Dated parenthetical decision annotations were removed from the active contract and are preserved in the canonical snapshot above.
